- madam president, we have all known for a long time that the pur sang intergovernmental conference is no longer a good method of putting forward a proper amendment to the treaty. the fact that a different approach was agreed at the laeken summit is encouraging. the convention was held above the baptismal font and its agenda is much broader than the four points imposed by nice. that is a merit of the belgian presidency, and i would like to congratulate that presidency. the quality of the triumvirate that will lead the convention and its bureau also offers perspective. no one will blame me for basing my confidence in this leadership mainly on the presence in this trio of former prime minister and party colleague jean-luc dehaene. mr president, the laeken declaration contains more than 60 non-rhetorical questions. although everyone who has been involved with europe since amsterdam, and indeed since ioanina, knows what the questions are, no one had expected to find satisfactory and complete answers to them by now. but with many of us, we were looking for some directional indicators and i actually miss them a bit in that statement from laken: the european parliament has been a very active participant in the process of european integration. actually, with my belgian glasses on, i could put it this way: verhofstadt asks the questions, dehaene must now make sure the answers come. i have three questions to the council presidency, mr president, and i would like an answer to them. first of all: does the council believe that this declaration and the convention will enable you to deepen the european union, at least at the same time as the next wave of enlargement? secondly: i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter. thirdly: it is an excellent idea to publish the laeken declaration in dozens of european newspapers, but are you not afraid that people will also want answers to the questions and concrete policy measures before they can really feel at home in the ever-expanding europe? finally, mr president, i would like to say this: i asked you at the beginning of the belgian presidency to convene an ngo summit, preferably a few weeks before laeken. i did not receive an answer from you at that time in this sitting, but you did organise that summit. so this was not a matter of words, but of deeds. we have not been used to that in belgium for some time and that was a pleasant surprise, for which i thank you.
- madam president, allow me to begin with some military language in these times of war. mr president, you have been a lieutenant general of europe for the last six months, along with your colleagues from the federal government and the länder. unfortunately, you were the lieutenant general of a mexican army: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. you have noticed that in the last six months, mr prime minister, we have noticed it in our files here every day. you are proud of your success in laeken in bringing some order to that mexican army. but the council is not just a mexican army, it's also a gang of carpet salesmen: they wanted to settle the seat issue quickly, like real carpet dealers. it is a matter of pride to you, mr prime minister, that you have put a stop to this. we want to get rid of back rooms, we want to get rid of the secret meetings between officials, between diplomats, between 15 heads of government. we want to move towards an open method, we want to give the hopeful method of the convention a chance, we want to open the doors and let a new wind blow. you asked the convention the right questions. you are right to say that the debate should be about the distribution of powers between the various levels of policy. you are also right to ask whether the day-to-day management of the union's policies could not be better left to the regions where the constitution so provides. but, mr prime minister, may i also express some criticism? for example, you did not ask how constitutional regions such as flanders, wallonia, the basque country, catalonia, scotland and wales could be explicitly involved in european decision-making. you are ignoring the demands of your party colleague patrick dewael, the prime minister of flanders, who published an important document on this subject with 50 colleagues in may and october. as regards the composition of the convention, three points. first of all: a triumvirate without a single woman, is that even from this time? no, mr prime minister. secondly: there is an imbalance in the composition of the convention between the members of the european parliament on the one hand and the national parliaments on the other. i think you are making a mistake here, the council is making a mistake here: i think that the european parliament is the institution par excellence where the european idea, where the european general interest prevails, and you are missing an opportunity here. finally, as regards the composition of the convention, the representatives of the constitutional regions cannot participate directly in the convention. they must follow the committee of the regions' lead as an observer, and i find that regrettable. in any case, mr prime minister, you can be sure that i and my party, with the nine representatives of the european free alliance, will contribute constructively to this convention, because europe must not become a mass of unity, europe must remain a sparkling diamond with many facets, facets of diversity, of its own identity, because only then can the european diamond shine in the european firmament.
- i believe that the european summit, with the laeken declaration, has in fact made a very appropriate follow-up to six months of the belgian presidency. it became a catalogue of empty words, of meaninglessness, of unanswered questions. it is very significant for the course of events in this europe that only someone like former commissioner van miert has dared to put his finger on these wounds. what louis michel dared to call the "grand cru verhofstadt" turns out to be table wine with a cork flavor. the european parliament has been the subject of a number of reports, and i am sure that the commission will be able to give its opinion on them. but that convention cannot make us forget that the principle of subsidiarity is being systematically violated in our institutions, that no referendums are being held on very radical measures and that the european union is even violating the democratic principle of the sovereignty of the member states again at this summit, for example by once again ignoring the results of the irish referendum in a very blatant manner. european democracy apparently means that citizens should not be allowed to take part in decision-making, and if they are allowed to vote, they should vote, as was the case in denmark and as is also required of ireland, until they agree with what the eurocracy has decided. it is possible that president verhofstadt will go down in history, but it will be as the man who went so far that even president prodi refused to hold a press conference with him, as the once flemish-minded politician who refused to protect the dutch language, as the man who threw all his principles overboard, who once made himself known as a supporter of the light state and deregulation but who became the partisan of european centralism of the superstate that made the invention of rules a kind of olympic discipline.
- christmas or not, there's something in a text that says it, but there's also something it doesn't say. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a directive on the protection of the environment against the risks arising from the use of radioactive substances. perhaps more dangerous is the persistent silence on the general budgetary implications of the transformation of community europe, which portends difficult meetings with the people. and yet the atmosphere which has been created by the laeken declaration is undeniably positive. this means that the positive points are important compared to those that are not in the results. others like myself have pointed out and will continue to point out that the belgian presidency, ambitious in its objectives and tenacious in its action, did not go off the rails, nor did the euro, either on 11 september or afterwards. for those of you who know international relations, it's huge. this is a huge step not only for belgium but for the whole of the union. mr prime minister, we appreciate the personal role you have played in the solution to the almost scandalous problem of the arrest warrant, from which it would not have been appropriate for the sitting prime ministers and heads of state to have been exempted. it is also clear that the socialists are particularly sensitive to progress in the social field: confirmation of the role of structural indicators in the field of employment, support for the european social model, particularly through cooperation in the field of pensions, better perception of exclusion and social inclusion, are important achievements for the citizens of europe. i come now to the convention, which has already attracted the attention of so many colleagues, and which i would simply like to say how much today's statements show that it will have to face a formidable task. you have therefore given priority to its composition and, with one exception, the decisions taken, and especially the innovations, are important achievements. the representation of the candidate countries is also included. full participation without the right to vote is more in keeping with the dignity of a state, and it is wise that this question has been linked, in the texts at any rate, to that of the regions with legislative powers, which are also recognized, contrary to what has just been said, with the status they deserve. parliament is also sensitive to the role you are reserving for civil society. and i want to stress the value of the system of the presidential troika that you have chosen, which, in my view, responds in many ways to the needs of the time and far beyond the perspectives linked to the personalities selected, personalities that i do not think i would discuss, they are all three men of quality, but in this capacity, mr prime minister, they are men. and so, like others, in this time of hope, i would like to express the hope that the european summit may one day begin to learn in all the languages we want, to know better, to appreciate better, to decline better, perhaps even to conjugate better, at the threshold of the 21st century, a little word that no longer has a right to be cited not only in poetry but in politics, in french, the word "woman". - mr president, mr prime minister, after your presidency we are faced with a very different situation from one year ago: the atmosphere is completely different, we can start working and you have indeed put three men in charge. i am curious to know how many members the european parliament delegation will have at the convention. we'll count it when it's that far. you have put the questions on the table, a lot of questions and i think the right questions. maybe we can add one more here and there, why not. i think the convention should be able to have that freedom too. you sketched a dream earlier. i think that is a very good summary, a very good perspective for the things we should be doing, not just us, the western europeans, the 15, but also eastern europe and the rest of the world. i would like to ask you, mr president-in-office, one more thing. you've been chairman for six months now. the belgian government will not be president for seven years. i would ask you to convince all your ministers that european work is also of the utmost importance during these seven years, that the government must not only be concerned with europe during the presidency but also outside it, because that period is longer and certainly so important.
- mr president, we are here concluding the belgian presidency, and that should normally be done in the presence of the council. the belgian presidency has been complimented here on the fact that the ministers have been here so often, but now we are assessing their presidency without anyone else from the council being present. i wonder if this debate has any further purpose. who are we addressing here?
